# SPI link timing: PRU0 controller ↔ PRU1 edge-polling peripheral

Generated by `python tools/spi_sweep.py --write` on 2026-11-05T01:46:10+00:00 from `pru_io/spi_rates.json` (sweep wall time 132.0 s). Do not edit by hand. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-04-spi-pru0-pru1-design.md`.

## How a row is decided

For every mode, core clock and half period k (SPI CLK = core / 2k, 10–50 MHz, never 50 MHz at 200 MHz) and every hop delay, the sweep:

1. single-steps one PRU1 R/W frame and measures PRU1's work per bit and per byte, not counting poll iterations that loop back while waiting for an edge, and the idle-byte TX fetch;
2. reassembles PRU0 for every realisable `miso_delay` (`controller_delay_ok`: `d mod 2k` not 0 or k, pipelining ≤ 6 bits) until the passing window has closed, one R/W frame each, and picks the passing value nearest the middle of the window, the earlier one on a tie (Ruling R9; the window has holes at `d mod 2k ∈ {0, k}`);
3. on a fresh simulator at that delay runs the spec §9 acceptance sequence: WRITE, READ of a preloaded TX block and R/W full duplex with every pattern (a5_5a, walking1, walking0, count, toggle), an unknown command (0x04), an out-of-range address (0x80), a mid-frame /CS abort after 76 bits, and two back-to-back R/W frames. Every MISO byte PRU0 receives, every MOSI byte on the bus, all 128 PRU1 RX blocks and the PRU1 status counters are checked.

**Supported** = zero byte errors and every step right at hop delay **5 cycles** on both cores (Ruling R6). Rows at hop delay 0 are reported for comparison only.

## Sources and modelling assumptions

- AM243 data only from SPRSP65J (AM243x datasheet) §6.11.5.16.1:
  - Table 6-112 (PRU timing conditions): input slew rate SR<sub>I</sub> 1–3 V/ns; output load C<sub>L</sub> 2–30 pF.
  - Table 6-113 (direct output mode), PRDO1: t<sub>sk(GPO-GPO)</sub> skew GPO to GPO, max 2 ns.
  - There is no direct-input-mode timing table. Input setup and hold rows are therefore a **design budget**: measured value − 2 ns output skew (PRDO1) − one core cycle of polling uncertainty. They are not datasheet limits.
- Hop delay: **5 core cycles** pin to pin, applied on the GPI input side of both cores at every core clock. It comes from **one scope measurement at 333 MHz**: `wbs` on an input followed by `set` on an output took **18 ns** (6 cycles) on silicon, against 1 cycle in the simulator with no delay. The split between input synchroniser, pads and output path is **unknown**; since every SPI net is one output to one input, putting the whole delay on the input side is equivalent here. At core clocks below 333 MHz, 5 cycles is more than 15 ns, so the model is cautious there.
- Board trace and jumper-wire delay between the LP-AM243 headers are **not modelled** (a few cm of wire is well under 1 ns).
- The simulator models no pad skew: t<sub>sk</sub> is shown as 0 in sim and is a derived row (never the worst).
- "Measured in sim" comes from the bus monitor on PRU0's pins, last back-to-back frame pair (first frame; t<sub>CSH</sub> is the gap to the second). The MISO setup/hold rows refer PRU0's sample to its MISO pin (Ruling R7a): the delayed R31 read at launch edge + `miso_delay` sees the pin as it was one hop delay earlier, so the pin-referred sample point is launch + `miso_delay` − hop. The MOSI rows need no such correction, because the hop delays CLK and MOSI equally at PRU1. A MISO change is assigned to the latest launch edge at least one hop before it (PRU1 sees each edge one hop late), so t<sub>V,MISO</sub> includes the inbound hop and does not wrap when the sample is pipelined, as long as PRU1 answers within one bit period (it does in every passing row: its MISO bytes are right).
- Simulator limitation (recorded, not fixed): a GPI change that arrives while the reading core is inside a multi-cycle instruction is stamped at the end of that instruction (`io_port._record_gpi` uses the reader's cycle counter), so it can land late and two edges inside one stall can merge. The PRU0↔PRU1 runs here are not affected: PRU1's longest in-frame instruction is a 3-cycle `lbbo` (stepped), shorter than any half period, and PRU0 does no loads or stores inside a frame.

## Controller /CS-high gap

The spec minimum gap between frames is 2 t<sub>CLK</sub> (4k cycles). PRU0's controller first holds /CS high for at least 4k cycles, then realigns and stores the MISO samples, updates the control block and polls `go` again, so the shortest gap it produces is longer than 2 t<sub>CLK</sub>. The back-to-back step sets `go` for the second frame as soon as the first reports done, i.e. at that controller minimum (column t<sub>CSH</sub> cycles below, measured on the bus): the controller's real gap is **135–249 cycles** over the sweep, always ≥ 4k. The strict 2 t<sub>CLK</sub> gap is therefore not exercised PRU0 to PRU1; it is proven for PRU1 by the Task 4 direct-stimulus tests: `tests/test_spi_peripheral.py::test_min_cs_gap_no_frame_lost` drives PRU1's GPI directly at a 4k-cycle gap (k = 3 and 6, both modes, hop 5). The minimum PRU1 needs is 9 cycles (mode 1) / 5 (mode 0) at k = 3.

## PRU1 cycles (single-stepped)

| mode | data bit (polls + MISO drive) | data bit (max, with gap work) | steady data byte | max byte 0–17 | idle-byte TX fetch |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 4 | 6 | 40 | 47 | 12 |
| 1 | 5 | 6 | 47 | 48 | 12 |

PRU1's work does not depend on the rate (it waits for each edge); the numbers are the same in every row. A byte lasts 16k cycles; the PRU1 slack per byte is 16k − max byte. At k = 3 (48 cycles) that is the tightest PRU1 budget.

Limiting instruction sequence (the per-bit polls of `source/spi/spi_peripheral.asm`, stepped in `tests/test_spi_peripheral.py::test_per_bit_cycles`): mode 1 data bit = launch-edge poll 2 + MISO drive (`lsl r30.w0`) 1 + capture-edge poll 2 = 5 cycles; a data byte adds the 7-op gather (xor/shift) of the 8 capture snapshots, 47 cycles. Mode 0 data bit = capture poll 2 + launch poll 1 + MISO drive 1 = 4 cycles; a byte adds the gather and one `xor 0xFF`, 40 cycles. The max-byte column also counts the TX fetch and commit-pointer work placed in the gaps of the header bytes. Every poll is a single masked R31 read plus a branch, so a bit cannot cost fewer than two cycles per edge; PRU1 was not optimised further because no catalogue rate is limited by it (see below).

## Fastest supported rate per mode

- Mode 0: **50.000 MHz** at 300 MHz, k = 3 (`m0_c300_k3_h5`). Limits: MISO window 4 values [13..17]; PRU1 slack 1 of 48 cycles/byte; worst margin tSU,MISO 1.33 ns.
  - every mode 0 combination of the test matrix passes; a faster rate would need k < 3 or > 50 MHz, outside the matrix. PRU1 still has 1 cycle(s) of slack per byte at k = 3; the MISO window is the narrowest there.
- Mode 1: **50.000 MHz** at 300 MHz, k = 3 (`m1_c300_k3_h5`). Limits: MISO window 2 values [16..17]; PRU1 slack 0 of 48 cycles/byte; worst margin tSU,MISO 1.33 ns.
  - every mode 1 combination of the test matrix passes; a faster rate would need k < 3 or > 50 MHz, outside the matrix. PRU1 itself is exactly at its limit at k = 3 (0 cycles slack per byte), and the MISO window is the narrowest there.
